Boats arriving from Tangyang pay full inward dues if discharging at Shasi town, local trade dues if discharging near the West station; but in the Shipping table they are all entered as local traders. In the same way boats from Shasi town to Tangyang pay full outward dues, but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table first as local entries, then as interport clearances. Boats arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local clearances. 11.-IM PORTS. 10. PART III. NOTIFICA TIONS During the Quarter ended 31st March 1912.
282 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JAN.-MAR., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 521. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. STEEP ISLAND RESPONDING FOG-GUN SIGNAL TO BE DISCONTINUED. REFERRING to Notice to Mariners No. 510, notice is hereby given that reports from vessels which have passed Steep Island during fog show that the siren was first heard by them at the following distances:- From the southward, at I, 3, 4, 6, 7, and Ii miles; and from the northward, at I, 7, and 7 miles. From about the 22nd January the responding fog-gun signal at this station will be discontinued. SHANGHAI, 4th January 1912. No. 522 TIENTSIN DISTRICT. NORTH FORT LIGHT SHIFTED. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to the washing away of the river bank, the North Fort Light has been shifted 347 feet N. I z&#39; W., magnetic, from its former position. SHANGHAI, 16th JARmanul 1912.
1912.] NOTIoICAIONS. 288 No. 523. SIGNALS MADE BY LIGHT-VESSELS WHEN OUT OF POSITION &#39;NOTIE is hereby given that, from the 15th Febinary 19g2, the following uniform system of signals to indicate that a light-vessel is out of position .will become operative as regards all light-vessels of the Chinese Service:- By day: a red flag hoisted at the masthead or yard-arm. By night: the usual light discontinued, and a red light exhibited from each end of the vessel. In the case of a light-vessel whose usual light is red, or red and white, a white light instead of a red light will be exhibited from each end of the vessel. SANHI, 2 3rd January 19 12. No. 526.. KIUNlGCHOW DISTRICT. IIAINAN STRAIT. UNHARED ROCK OFF HAINAN HAD. NOTIo is hereby given that the s.s. Shanwag is reported to have struck an uncharted rook off Hainan Head. From the rock, Hainan Bluff (highest part) bore 8. 31* E., magnetic, distant 8 uables. It is estimated that the depth of water over the rook is I8 feet at low water of spring tides. SHANGHAI, 2nd February 1912.
284 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JAN.-MAR., No. 525. SANTUAO DISTRICT. KAISER ROCK BUOY SHIFTED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Kaiser Rock Buoy has been shifted. From the new position of the buoy, the Kaiser Rock bears S. 470 W., and distant 1.9 cables. SHANGHAI, 12th March 1912. No. 526. KONGMOON DISTRICT. ALTERATIONS TO WANGMOON BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Wangmoon Beacon Light is now exhibited from a pole above a white hut erected on screw piles. The pole is surmounted by a black spherical shape. The light, which is elevated 34 feet above high water, is dioptric, of the seventh order, showing a fixed red light, which should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 4 miles. SHANGHAI, 19th March 1912.
1912.] NOTIFICATIONS. 285 No. 527. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. YANGTZE RIVER-CONFUCIUS CHANNEL AND LANGSHAN CROSSING. ALTERATIONS IN BUOYAGE, AND ESTABLISHMENT OF PLOVER POINT Buoy. REFERRING to Special Notice to Mariners No. 366, notice is hereby given that the changes in aids to navigation notified therein have been carried out. The Dove&#39;s Nest Upper Buoy is now moored in 50 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Leo Point Beacon bears S. S1o W., distant 2.27 miles. The Actaeon Buoy is now moored in 37 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Leo Point Beacon bears S. 22&#39; E., distant 4.7 miles. A 6-foot conical buoy, painted red and surmounted by a cylindrical shape, and known as the Plover Point Buoy, has been moored in 38 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 29-* W., distant 11.29 miles. This buoy marks the starboard side of the channel for vessels bound up river. The Middle Bank Buoy has been changed in colour to red, and is now moored in 54 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 142&quot; W., distant 10.2 miles. This buoy now marks the starboard side of the channel for vessels bound up river. The North Bank Buoy is now moored in 34 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. I1 E., distant 10.19 miles. The Langshan Buoy is now moored in 44 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 16&#39; E., distant 9.36 miles. The Upper Crossing Buoy is now moored in 45 feet of water at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Langshan Pagoda bears N. 581* E., distant 5.88 miles. Unless otherwise stated, the characteristics of these buoys remain unchanged. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 29th March 1912.
